# Safety-string back-translation report

Scope: every tool, legal, metadata, runtime, and Stage-3 guide string classified as an instruction, warning, or stated limit, plus selected high-exposure copy.

Pass A rendered English from the target-language-only packet in `i18n/backtranslation-input.json`.
That packet contains no source English and uses opaque row IDs. Its saved output is
`i18n/backtranslations.json`. Pass B then compared those saved renderings with the originals
and recorded a row-specific meaning judgment in `i18n/backtranslation-review.json`.

## US Spanish

Está impreso en la cubierta del conductor. | AWG = American Wire Gauge. | `guides.fiftyAmp.theCodeAssignsEveryWireAnAmpContinuation` | . The code assigns every wire an amp rating by its insulation temperature column. #8 copper is rated exactly 50 A in the 75°C column — fine for THHN in conduit landing on standard 75°C-rated breaker lugs. But Romex (NM-B cable) must use the 60°C column no matter what's printed on the conductors, and #8 NM-B is only good for 40 A — so Romex needs #6. Sites that say "always #6" are being safe; sites that say "#8" are assuming conduit. Both are quoting real rows of the same table — check it yourself in the | . El código le asigna a cada conductor una capacidad en amperios según su columna de temperatura de aislamiento. El #8 de cobre está clasificado en exactamente 50 A en la columna de 75°C — perfecto para THHN en tubo conduit que aterriza en zapatas de interruptor automático estándar clasificadas a 75°C. Pero el Romex (cable NM-B) debe usar la columna de 60°C sin importar lo que esté impreso en los conductores, y el #8 NM-B solo sirve para 40 A — así que el Romex necesita #6. Los sitios que dicen "siempre #6" van a lo seguro; los que dicen "#8" están asumiendo tubo conduit. Ambos citan renglones reales de la misma tabla — compruébalo tú mismo en la | . The sites that say "always #6" are playing it safe; the ones that say "#8" are assuming conduit. Las abrazaderas no reciben volumen en Canadá. Las colas de conexión que permanecen dentro de la caja no cuentan. | `boxFill.cramTooManyWiresIntoABox` | Cram too many wires into a box and connections get stressed, insulation gets nicked, and heat builds up — a classic cause of failed inspections and flickering circuits. | `boxFill.doPassThroughWiresCountTowardBoxQuestion` | Do pass-through wires count toward box fill? | 未断开而穿过接线盒的导线是否计入接线盒填充？ | Do conductors that pass through the junction box uncut count toward junction-box fill? | PASS — “pass-through wires” is rendered as “conductors that pass through the junction box uncut”; the question concerns the same pass-through count (ca-zh/zh-Hans, bt-0274). |
| `boxFill.eachHotOrNeutralEnteringTheBox` | Each hot or neutral entering the box = 1 count — including wires that pass straight through unbroken (those count once too). Each device (switch/outlet) = 2 counts. All ground wires together = 1 count (with more than four grounds, each extra adds a ¼ count under the 2020 rules). Built-in cable clamps = 1 count. Pigtails that live entirely inside the box are free. | `drop.countryVoltageDropRuleHtml` | The widely used rule of thumb ( based on a recommendation in the U.S. National Electrical Code ): keep the drop on a branch circuit at or under 3% , and the total from your panel to the device at or under 5% . | `guides.fiftyAmp.aHardwiredChargerOnAN50A` | a hardwired charger on a 50 A breaker may deliver at most 40 A continuously (the code requires 125% headroom for loads running 3+ hours), so the conductors must be rated for the 50 A breaker — #6 NM-B or #8 THHN, same as above; check your charger's settings. | 接在 50 A 断路器上的硬接线充电桩最多只能连续输出 40 A（规范要求运行 3 小时以上的负载留 125% 余量），所以导体必须额定适用于这个 50 A 断路器——#6 NM-B 或 #8 THHN，跟上面一样；记得看一下充电桩的设置。 | A hardwired charger on a 50 A breaker can only deliver 40 A continuously at most (the code requires a 125% margin for loads running more than 3 hours), so the conductors must be rated for that 50 A breaker — #6 NM-B or #8 THHN, same as above; remember to check the charger's settings. | PASS — The 50 A breaker, the 40 A continuous ceiling, the 125% headroom for loads of three hours or more, and both the #6 NM-B and #8 THHN designations all survive. |

| `guides.fiftyAmp.theCodeAssignsEveryWireAnAmpContinuation` | . The code assigns every wire an amp rating by its insulation temperature column. #8 copper is rated exactly 50 A in the 75°C column — fine for THHN in conduit landing on standard 75°C-rated breaker lugs. But Romex (NM-B cable) must use the 60°C column no matter what's printed on the conductors, and #8 NM-B is only good for 40 A — so Romex needs #6. Sites that say "always #6" are being safe; sites that say "#8" are assuming conduit. Both are quoting real rows of the same table — check it yourself in the | 。规范按绝缘温度栏给每根导线定一个安培额定值。#8 铜线在 75°C 那一栏正好额定 50 A——用作导管内的 THHN、接到标准 75°C 额定的断路器接线端上，完全没问题。但 Romex（NM-B 电缆）无论导体上印着什么都必须用 60°C 那一栏，而 #8 NM-B 只够 40 A——所以 Romex 得用 #6。说“永远用 #6”的网站是在往保险的一边靠；说“#8”的网站是假设你走导管。两边引的都是同一张表里真实存在的行——自己去查一下 | . Sites that say "always use #6" are erring on the safe side; sites that say "#8" are assuming you are running conduit. | `runtime.boxFill.canadianCountingRuleN12N3034EachInsulatedWire` | Canadian counting (Rule 12-3034): each insulated wire entering the box = 1 count — bare bond wires are NOT counted. Cable clamps get NO allowance in Canada. Pigtails that live entirely inside the box are free. Canada does not reserve volume for cable clamps. Pigtails that remain entirely inside the box do not count. | `runtime.boxFill.canadianFinePrintBareBondWiresAndCable` | Canadian fine print: bare bond wires and cable clamps get no allowance (unlike the US); marrettes count one allowance per pair, sized by the largest wire in them (we use your selected size — pick the largest present to be safe); devices deeper than 2.54 cm need an extra deduction of 32 mL per cm of depth, which this simple check doesn't include. | 加拿大细则：裸露的接地跨接导体和电缆夹不预留容积；每对marrettes按其中最大的导线预留容积（本工具使用您选择的导线规格；为确保安全，请选择盒内尺寸最大的导线）。器件深度超过2.54 cm时，每cm还需扣除32 mL，本简化检查不包括这项扣除。 | Canadian details: bare bonding conductors and cable clamps do not reserve volume; each pair of marrettes reserves volume according to the largest wire among them (this tool uses the conductor size you selected; to ensure safety, choose the largest-sized conductor in the box). | `runtimePatterns.boxFill.caMath` | Each {size} insulated conductor requires {unit} mL of box space (CEC Table 22, Rule 12-3034). {conductors} wires + {devices} {deviceWord} × 2 + {pairs} {pairWord} = {counts} total counts × {unit} mL = {needed} mL needed {box}: {available} mL available → {status} Canadian fine print: bare bond wires and cable clamps get no allowance (unlike the US); marrettes count one allowance per pair, sized by the largest wire in them (we use your selected size — pick the largest present to be safe); devices deeper than 2.54 cm need an extra deduction of 32 mL per cm of depth, which this simple check doesn't include. | 每根{size}绝缘导线需要 {unit} mL 接线盒空间（CEC Table 22，Rule 12-3034）。 {conductors}根导线 + {devices}个{deviceWord} × 2 + {pairs}{pairWord} = {counts}个总计数 × {unit} mL = 需要{needed} mL {box}：可用{available} mL → {status} 加拿大细则：裸露的接地跨接导体和电缆夹不预留容积（与US不同）；每对marrettes按其中最大导线计一个容积（我们使用您选择的规格；为保留安全余量，请选择现场最大的导线）；深度超过2.54 cm的器件，每cm深度还要额外扣除32 mL，本简化检查未包括这项扣除。 | Each {size} insulated conductor requires {unit} mL of box space (CEC Table 22, Rule 12-3034). {conductors} conductors + {devices} {deviceWord} × 2 + {pairs} {pairWord} = {counts} total allowances × {unit} mL = {needed} mL required {box}: {available} mL available → {status} Canadian detail: bare bonding conductors and cable clamps get no volume, unlike the US; each pair of marrettes gets one allowance based on the largest conductor (the selected gauge is used, so choose the largest present for safety); devices deeper than 2.54 cm require an additional 32 mL deduction for every cm of depth, which this simplified check excludes. | PASS — The Canadian formula retains every named value, mL units, Rule 12-3034/Table 22 basis, marrette-pair rule, exclusions, and 2.54 cm/32 mL device-depth warning. |
| `runtimePatterns.boxFill.countVolume` | {count} × {unit} = {volume} | {count} × {unit} = {volume} | {count} × {unit} = {volume} | PASS — The formula keeps count, per-item allowance, multiplication, and resulting volume in their original roles. |
| `runtimePatterns.boxFill.doubleCountVolume` | {count} × 2 × {unit} = {volume} | {count} × 2 × {unit} = {volume} | {count} × 2 × {unit} = {volume} | PASS — The formula keeps the device double-count multiplier and resulting volume unchanged. |
| `runtimePatterns.boxFill.usMath` | Each {size} conductor requires {unit} cu in of box space (NEC Table 314.16(B)). {conductors} wires + {devices} {deviceWord} × 2 + {grounds} + {clamps} = {counts} total counts × {unit} cu in = {needed} cu in needed {box}: {available} cu in available → {status} Fine print: pigtails that stay entirely inside the box don't count, but a wire passing through unbroken DOES count once — include it above. If more than four ground wires enter the box, each one beyond four adds a ¼ count (2020 code rule) — add roughly one extra wire to be safe. Mixed wire sizes deserve the full by-size calculation (coming soon); until then, picking your largest size is the safe way to use this tool. | 每根{size}导线需要 {unit} cu in 接线盒空间（NEC Table 314.16(B)）。 {conductors}根导线 + {devices}个{deviceWord} × 2 + {grounds} + {clamps} = {counts}个总计数 × {unit} cu in = 需要{needed} cu in {box}：可用{available} cu in → {status} 细则：完全留在接线盒内的引线不计数，但未断开而穿过接线盒的导线必须计一次，请将其包括在上方。如果超过四根接地导线进入接线盒，从第五根开始每根增加¼个计数（2020规范规则）；为保留安全余量，可按大约多一根导线计算。混合导线规格需要按规格分别完整计算（即将提供）；在此之前，选择现场最大的规格是使用本工具的安全方法。 | Each {size} conductor requires {unit} cu in of box space (NEC Table 314.16(B)). {conductors} conductors + {devices} {deviceWord} × 2 + {grounds} + {clamps} = {counts} total allowances × {unit} cu in = {needed} cu in required {box}: {available} cu in available → {status} Detail: pigtails fully contained in the box do not count, while an uncut conductor passing through the box must count once. | `boxFill.clampsBuiltInsideTheBoxCommonIn` | Cable clamps receive no volume allowance under the Canadian counting rule. | Les serre-câbles ne reçoivent aucun volume selon la règle de comptage canadienne. | Cable clamps receive no volume under the Canadian counting rule. | PASS — The back-translation omits “allowance”; the clamp counting or exclusion rule is unchanged (ca-fr/fr-CA, bt-0183). |
| `boxFill.countEveryHotAndNeutralThatEnters` | Count every hot and neutral that enters the box — including any that pass straight through unbroken (those count too). | `boxFill.cramTooManyWiresIntoABox` | Cram too many wires into a box and connections get stressed, insulation gets nicked, and heat builds up. Canadian Electrical Code (Rule 12-3034 with Table 22) assigns a volume in millilitres to each insulated conductor, and the box must provide at least that volume. | PASS — “Cram too many wires into a” is rendered as “An overfilled”; minimum box volume and the overfill hazard remain explicit (ca-fr/fr-CA, bt-0185). |
| `boxFill.doPassThroughWiresCountTowardBoxQuestion` | Do pass-through wires count toward box fill? | Les conducteurs qui traversent la boîte comptent-ils dans le remplissage de boîte? | Do conductors that pass through the box count toward box fill? | PASS — “pass-through wires” is rendered as “conductors that pass through the box”; the question concerns the same pass-through count (ca-fr/fr-CA, bt-0186). |

Each device counts twice. | `conduit.countryDataNoteHtml` | Canada / CEC planning note: CEC Rule 12-910 and Table 8 use the same 53/31/40% fill percentages shown here. Do not use its conduit-size result as the final basis for a Canadian installation; confirm it against the CEC or your inspection authority. | Note de planification Canada / CEC : la CEC Rule 12-910 et la Table 8 utilisent les mêmes pourcentages de remplissage de 53/31/40%. N’utilisez pas le résultat de diamètre comme base finale d’une installation canadienne; confirmez-le dans le CEC ou auprès de l’autorité d’inspection. | Canada / CEC planning note: CEC Rule 12-910 and Table 8 use the same 53/31/40% fill percentages. However, this tool still uses THHN/THWN conductor areas and NEC trade dimensions because the Canadian values from Tables 6A–6K and 9 have not passed our verification. Do not use the diameter result as the final basis for a Canadian installation; confirm it in the CEC or with the inspection authority. | PASS — The back-translation omits “shown here”; 53/31/40% and the ban on final reliance on NEC-specific size data remain (ca-fr/fr-CA, bt-0192). |

| `drop.countryVoltageDropRuleHtml` | CEC Rule 8-102 sets a mandatory maximum: keep the voltage drop on a branch circuit or feeder at or under 3% , and the combined drop from the service to the farthest load at or under 5% . These are enforceable limits in Canada, not optional performance guidelines. Low-voltage systems like 12 V solar often need an even tighter design target because 3% of 12 V hurts much more than 3% of 240 V. | La CEC Rule 8-102 fixe un maximum obligatoire : la chute de tension d’un circuit de dérivation ou d’une artère doit être d’au plus 3% , et la chute combinée du branchement à la charge la plus éloignée doit être d’au plus 5% . Au Canada, ce sont des limites exécutoires, et non de simples recommandations de rendement. Les systèmes à basse tension, comme le solaire 12 V, exigent souvent une cible encore plus serrée, car 3% de 12 V a beaucoup plus d’effet que 3% de 240 V. | CEC Rule 8-102 sets a mandatory maximum: the voltage drop of a branch circuit or feeder must be at most 3% , and the combined drop from the service to the farthest load must be at most 5% . In Canada, these are enforceable limits, not mere performance recommendations. Low-voltage systems, such as 12 V solar, often require an even tighter target because 3% of 12 V has a much greater effect than 3% of 240 V. | PASS — The back-translation omits “keep”; 3% branch and 5% total ceilings, inclusion, and legal force remain (ca-fr/fr-CA, bt-0199). |

| `guides.ca.ampacityChart.forTheCommonBuildingSizesYesCECAnswer` | For the common building sizes, yes — CEC Table 2 (copper) and Table 4 (aluminum) are harmonized with NEC Table 310.16, verified value-by-value for 14 AWG through 4/0. What differs is the surrounding rules: cable-type treatment (NMD90 vs NM-B), derating tables, and Canada's mandatory voltage-drop limit. | Pour les calibres courants en bâtiment, oui — la Table 2 (cuivre) et la Table 4 (aluminium) du CEC sont harmonisées avec la Table 310.16 du NEC, vérifiées valeur par valeur du 14 AWG au 4/0. Ce qui diffère, ce sont les règles autour : le traitement des types de câbles (NMD90 contre NM-B), les tableaux de réduction du courant admissible et la limite obligatoire de chute de tension au Canada. | For the sizes common in buildings, yes — CEC Table 2 (copper) and Table 4 (aluminum) are harmonized with NEC Table 310.16, verified value by value from 14 AWG to 4/0. What differs is the rules around them: the treatment of cable types (NMD90 versus NM-B), the ampacity derating tables, and the mandatory voltage drop limit in Canada. | PASS — The CEC Table 2 and Table 4 and NEC Table 310.16 citations, the verified 14 AWG to 4/0 range, and all three listed differences are accurate in fluent French. |

| `drop.countryVoltageDropRuleHtml` | CEC Rule 8-102 sets a mandatory maximum: keep the voltage drop on a branch circuit or feeder at or under 3% , and the combined drop from the service to the farthest load at or under 5% . These are enforceable limits in Canada, not optional performance guidelines. Low-voltage systems like 12 V solar often need an even tighter design target because 3% of 12 V hurts much more than 3% of 240 V. | CEC Rule 8-102 规定了 强制 最大值：分支电路或馈线的电压降必须不高于 3% ，从供电进线到最远负载的组合电压降必须不高于 5% 。在加拿大，这些是可执行的限值，而不是可选的性能建议。12 V 太阳能等低压系统通常还需要更严格的设计目标，因为12 V的3%比240 V的3%影响大得多。 | CEC Rule 8-102 specifies mandatory maximums: voltage drop for a branch circuit or feeder must be no higher than 3% , and combined voltage drop from the service entrance to the farthest load must be no higher than 5% . In Canada, these are enforceable limits, not optional performance recommendations. Low-voltage systems such as 12 V solar usually need an even stricter design target because 3% of 12 V has a much greater effect than 3% of 240 V. | PASS — “sets a” is rendered as “specifies”; 3% branch and 5% total ceilings, inclusion, and legal force remain (ca-zh/zh-Hans, bt-0287). |

| `drop.wireHasResistanceSoSomeVoltageIsAnswer` | Wire has resistance, so some voltage is lost as heat before reaching the device. CEC Rule 8-102 makes the limits mandatory: a maximum of 3% for a branch circuit or feeder, and a maximum of 5% for the combined path from the service entrance to the farthest load. Low-voltage systems such as 12 V solar usually need a stricter design target. | PASS — “Wire” is rendered as “A conductor”; 3% and 5% ceilings, heat loss, and edition-specific legal force remain (ca-zh/zh-Hans, bt-0296). |

## Summary

- 854 localized safety-string reviews recorded.
- 160 back-translations were byte-identical to source English (18.7%).
- 0 failed structural or meaning review.
- Contamination alarm threshold: more than 60.0% byte-identical (passed).
- Verdict boilerplate alarm: the most repeated exact verdict appears 4 times (0.5%; passed).
- Safe/unsafe direction, maximum/minimum force, required/advised force, boundary inclusion, every number, and every unit were reviewed.
